AVGO — Evidence appendix
All harvested proof points · company first, then peers

Evidence

Claims by section
business-model
  • In FY2025 the two produced $36,858m and $27,029m of revenue at 57.6% and 76.8% segment operating margins. By the quarter ended 3 May 2026 semiconductors had grown to 68% of the company.
  • From FY2023 to FY2025 AI added $16,390m while non-AI subtracted $7,714m, so AI supplied 189% of segment growth.
  • Semiconductor revenue equals accelerator units multiplied by accelerator price, plus networking content attached to those units, plus non-AI end-market demand.
  • Accelerator revenue grows about 2.7x from FY2025 to FY2026 while units grow about 1.1-1.3x, so most of the increase is price per unit
  • That combination produced a seven-year realized incremental operating margin of 67% (FY2018 to FY2025, $11,979m of additional segment operating income on $17,790m of additional revenue), and the AI-era quarters are landing at 66-68%, the same rate the segment delivered through the 2021-22 non-AI upcycle.
  • Consolidated R&D of $5,960m for the first half of FY2026 against segment R&D of $3,118m implies $2,842m of stock compensation inside R&D alone; allocating the semiconductor share of that plus SG&A takes the half-year segment operating margin from the filed 61.0% to roughly 53.7%
  • One term swings segment revenue, and it is set at signature. Arrangements without termination-for-convenience rights let Broadcom recognize the licence portion upfront on transfer of control; arrangements with those rights are recognized ratably.
  • Upfront licence revenue rose $3,199m in FY2025 against a segment revenue increase of $5,551m, so 58% of the year's incremental revenue came from a larger upfront-recognized component.
  • Broadcom's entire software segment — VMware plus mainframe plus Symantec plus enterprise software plus Fibre Channel SAN — spent $767m less on R&D in FY2025 than VMware alone spent in its final standalone year, on roughly twice the revenue, and the FQ2 FY2026 run-rate annualizes to $2,404m and is still declining.
  • Capital intensity is close to the lowest in the industry, because the leading-edge capital burden is at TSMC. Consolidated capital expenditure was $623m in FY2025, 1.0% of revenue and 1.09x that year's depreciation.
  • Unconditional, non-cancelable purchase commitments went from $132m at 2 November 2025 and $54m at 1 February 2026 to $128,110m at 3 May 2026, scheduled $22m in the FY2026 remainder, $55,214m in FY2027, $72,870m in FY2028 and $4m in FY2029.
competitive-advantage
  • Broadcom's semiconductor franchise earns returns far above any plausible cost of capital and has done so for eight consecutive years, and its durability is open to erosion by the six customers who now account for roughly half of it.
  • A customer starting from nothing needs two or three chips per node simply to find the flow's failure modes, by which time the node has moved on. That is a continuity advantage defended by out-executing, and it excludes nobody by contract or by law.
  • Six buyers now account for roughly half the company, each with the balance sheet and the internal silicon staff to fund a second source.
  • Merchant AI networking is a standard-parts business with a real standards-based network effect: Ethernet's value to a buyer rises with how many other buyers use it, because switch, NIC, optics and cable vendors all target the same standard, and Broadcom benefits from every hyperscaler that wants an alternative to a single vendor.
  • Adding segment-attributable receivables gives invested capital of roughly $33-37bn; allocating stock compensation on the segment's share of operating expense and taxing at management's guided 16% gives NOPAT of about $14.8bn, so segment return on invested capital is roughly 40-45%
  • And Credo at 68.5% and Astera at 73.3% show that a small focused entrant reaches Broadcom's gross margin; the 25-to-39-point operating-margin gap between them and Broadcom comes from R&D intensity of 20-36% against 6.7%, which is what the scale advantage buys.
  • On a basis that adds stock compensation back to NVIDIA and leaves Broadcom's segment margin as reported, Broadcom's semiconductor segment operating margin was 53.8% in FY2021 against NVIDIA's 35.6% in the year ended January 2021, a gap of 18.2 points in Broadcom's favour; in the latest calendar-aligned quarter Broadcom is at 61.8% against NVIDIA's 68.0%, a gap of 6.2 points the other way.
  • The closest public comparables are Cadence Design Systems and Synopsys, whose advantage is also a silicon IP library plus a design methodology re-earned at each process node and amortized across many customers, and whose incumbency comes from a customer's unwillingness to re-qualify a proven flow. The analogue breaks on revenue form: electronic design automation is licensed per seat and recurs whether or not a chip ships, while Broadcom's ASIC revenue is per programme, so a lost socket removes the whole stream at once.
  • A supplier that raises price several-fold and keeps nine tenths of its largest customers has priced against a switching cost those customers cannot pay to escape inside a renewal cycle.
  • Deducting an estimated $3.0-4.2bn of segment stock compensation and taxing at 16% gives NOPAT of $13.9-14.9bn, a return of 11.0% to 11.8% on what was paid
  • The growth that produced the FY2025 numbers came from a conversion that is finished. Penetration of the top 10,000 accounts moved from roughly 3,000 accounts to over 90% across five quarters, and reported segment growth then went from +26% for FY2025 to +1.4% in FQ1 FY2026 and +8.8% in FQ2.
  • Virtualization has functional substitutes that a database format or a mainframe instruction set does not.
  • The entire narrowing of the margin gap comes from Nutanix moving out of losses. The growth comparison is what changed: in the most recent reported quarters Nutanix grew faster than the segment it is attacking, on a base one tenth the size.
  • The advantage here is real and it is bounded by the renewal cycle: excess returns today, defensible through at least the FY2027-FY2028 renewals in the top 10,000 accounts, and eroding in the tail of the base where the price step has no offsetting value.
  • Roper Technologies and Constellation Software are the truest structural match: serial acquirers of embedded vertical software run for cash, with low organic growth and high margins funded by disciplined cost, buying an installed base instead of winning one.
competitive-landscape
  • Broadcom's share of Google's TPU volume is the largest unresolved quantity in the forward model. Published estimates span 65% to 95% and no filing gives a figure, so what follows describes the structure of the market and not Broadcom's position inside its largest programme.
  • On a content-adjusted basis Broadcom's share of the underlying silicon is higher than 11.4%, and neither company discloses the split that would let anyone say how much higher.
  • In merchant Ethernet switch silicon Broadcom is first on each generation and prices accordingly; it shipped the only 102 Tb/s switch for more than a year and taped out the 200 Tb/s part in FQ2 FY2026. In custom accelerators the customer owns the architecture, funds the programme and can re-bid each generation, so price is negotiated per generation.
  • Ten gigawatts at $15-20bn is $150-200bn, against guidance of "in excess of $100 billion" for fiscal 2027.
  • At the FQ4 FY2024 call in December 2024 it put the fiscal-2027 serviceable market for accelerators and networking across its three then-customers at $60-90bn and said Broadcom expected a leading share of it. Eighteen months later the company guides its own fiscal-2027 AI revenue above $100bn.
  • Alchip is the share loser and its case sizes the risk: revenue declined from $1,621m in CY2024 to $993m in CY2025 and a further 39% in the first half of CY2026 after Amazon's Annapurna took the majority-volume Trainium 3 tape-out in-house and began procuring from TSMC directly, while keeping Alchip on the smaller variant
  • Broadcom's leadership claim holds inside merchant Ethernet switch silicon, where it shipped the only 102 Tb/s part for over a year; NVIDIA's revenue is mostly NVLink and complete systems attached to its own racks, a socket Broadcom does not bid for.
  • Summing the identifiable virtualization-platform revenue gives roughly $30-38bn depending on how much of Microsoft's server line is attributed, of which Broadcom holds roughly 55-65% by revenue. Broadcom's workload share is lower than that revenue share, because its price per core is a multiple of the alternatives - Microsoft ships Hyper-V inside Windows Server and monetizes virtualization through the operating-system licence.
critical-points
  • Holding both segments at their FQ2 FY2026 gross margins of 69.5% and 93.0% and applying the guided revenue mix of roughly $20.5bn and $8.9bn produces a consolidated 76.6%. The guide is approximately 74%. Segment mix accounts for 0.5 of the 3.1-point decline
  • Two two-equation solves on filed segment revenue and filed segment cost of revenue, over period pairs in which the mix moved sharply, converge: FY2024 against FY2025 gives AI 70.5% and non-AI 65.3%; FQ2 FY2025 against FQ2 FY2026 gives 70.7% and 66.4%.
expectations
  • Consensus and management are on the same number for FY2027. They part company on what follows: consensus takes revenue to $345,140m by FY2030, a five-year compound rate of 40.1% from the FY2025 base, and in 865 company-years starting above $50bn of revenue the best five-year outcome observed is 29.3%.
  • Consensus has followed management and not led it: FY2027 revenue has doubled and FY2027 adjusted EPS has risen 93.1% over twelve months, tracking the sequence of upward revisions Broadcom issued on its own calls, and every revision in that period has been upward on revenue and EPS and downward on gross margin.
financial-history
  • GAAP gross margin rose 150 basis points year on year in FQ2 FY2026, from 68.0% to 69.5%. On the segment basis, which excludes acquisition-intangible amortization, it declined 230 basis points from 79.4% to 77.1%.
forward-model
  • The identity reproduces FY2025 to within $21m of the four disclosed quarters (2.3m units at $5,700 divided by 0.65 gives $20,169m against $20,190m) and FY2026 to within 1% of the guide (2.6m at $13,500 divided by 0.62 gives $56,613m against approximately $56,000m).
  • The base departs from management nowhere through FY2027: the FY2026 AI line is the $56bn guide plus the 1% median beat of the last seven AI guides, the FY2027 AI line of $109,701m falls between the ">$100 billion" floor and the roughly $112bn implied by Tan's "in the range of 2x", and FY2026 software holds the "low double-digit" framework.
m-and-a
  • Across roughly $166,000m of announced consideration and thirteen fiscal years there has been no goodwill impairment, with the annual fourth-quarter test concluding no impairment in every year from FY2015 to FY2025.
management-claims
  • The error has run in Broadcom's favour on margin each time, because networking is the richer half, where the residual solve puts gross margin at 75-80% against 64-68% for accelerators.
secular-trends
  • The non-AI half of the semiconductor segment has not grown in six years: above $16,570m in FY2019 and $16,668m in FY2025. Every dollar of semiconductor growth since FY2019 came from the AI line.
  • Of the roughly $40bn FY2026 revenue increment, approximately $36bn is AI semiconductors, approximately $3bn is infrastructure software, and approximately $0.9bn is non-AI. One line item, sold to six customers, disclosed only on earnings calls, supplies 90% of the growth.
  • In calendar 2025, the best year the semiconductor industry has had, Broadcom's semiconductor segment grew 22.4% while the Logic category grew 38.8%, so Broadcom's implied share of Logic went from 13.9% to 12.3%.
  • Broadcom's AI line grew at exactly NVIDIA's total rate in the calendar-2025 window, 65% against 65%, so on realized numbers the custom-silicon architecture took no share from the merchant GPU in the year it was supposed to.
  • Accelerating year-on-year growth, a book-to-bill near three, inventory days rising 28 in two quarters, and remaining performance obligations growing 3.7x in one quarter on a single contract describe a demand pull-forward.
technology
  • The flow is the product, and it is re-earned at each process node: Broadcom ports the flow to a new node, debugs it on the first two or three chips, then reuses the debugged flow for the next fifty to ninety.
  • This differentiation buys volume and visibility, and it does not convert to price.
  • Bandwidth has doubled every 18-24 months across five generations, and Broadcom has shipped ahead of its announced dates twice: Tomahawk 5 was guided in March 2023 to launch in early 2024 and shipped in late 2023, and Tomahawk 6 was targeted for late 2025 and shipped in June 2025.
  • Seven of the eight largest AI clusters in deployment used Broadcom Ethernet as of June 2024, and AI switch order backlog was above $10,000m at FY2025 year-end.
  • PCIe switching is a live challenge to a position Broadcom has held for twenty years across five generations.
  • Broadcom's position held where the process is proprietary and made in its own fab, and gave way where the technology was standard CMOS integration a well-resourced customer could reproduce.
  • Cash R&D grew 1.8% year on year in FY2025 while AI semiconductor revenue grew 65%.
  • What differentiates the segment is contract structure and the cost of migrating a production virtual-machine estate.
value-chain
  • Every input layer gained margin and every silicon and systems layer lost it or is guiding to lose it.
  • The evidence supports memory first, customer-side power second, and foundry third.

In FY2025 the two produced $36,858m and $27,029m of revenue at 57.6% and 76.8% segment operating margins. By the quarter ended 3 May 2026 semiconductors had grown to 68% of the company.

Our semiconductor and semiconductor-based solutions also enable our customers to build and deploy AI data center infrastructure for their training and inference workloads and manage the movement of data across their AI network infrastructure based on open, flexible, standards-based Ethernet. In addition, our solutions enable accelerated compute and networking connectivity at scale, within and across AI server racks and across AI data center sites. Customers of these solutions are hyperscalers and companies with AI frontier models, as well as original equipment manufacturers (“OEMs”) and system integrators that develop servers, switches and racks deployed in large-scale AI data centers to run training and inference workloads. Our AI semiconductor solutions include custom accelerators or XPUs, Ethernet switching and routing silicon, Ethernet NICs, physical layer devices (“PHYs”) and optical components, as well as racks and systems based on our XPUs.

presentationsupports
Custom AI silicon consists of 1, a large array of matrix multiplication and accumulation functions, and Broadcom implements this array with custom cell libraries and SRAMs in 5 nm or 3 nm. 2, Network connectivity is critical in AI systems for communication between nodes and the backbone networks. This is implemented with 100 Gig SerDes links, primarily over direct attached copper. Broadcom's 100 Gig SerDes is an important ingredient in realizing cost and power-optimized networks. 3, Broadcom's unique die-to-die interconnect with bandwidth greater than 10 terabits per second is used for partitioning the silicon and mixing die from different process technologies. 4, HBM2E or HBM3 provides the required bandwidth and capacity for AI workloads.
— Vijay Janapaty, VP & General Manager, Physical Layer Products Division, Broadcom / Deutsche Bank custom silicon accelerators teach-in (Status Update), 2022-04-19

Accelerator revenue grows about 2.7x from FY2025 to FY2026 while units grow about 1.1-1.3x, so most of the increase is price per unit

business-explainer · business-model · ↩ back to dossier

Company

earnings callcontradicts
So moving forward, we are going to move away from the inflexible perpetual license model for enterprise software to an enterprise-wide "all you can eat" license for all of our core accounts. By doing this, we expect to remove the friction caused by selling expensive upfront perpetual licenses so that the incremental costs for our customers to expand the use of enterprise products will be highly competitive relative to SaaS-based alternatives. Bottom line, we are adopting a fully ratable subscription model for the Broadcom software business.
— Hock E. Tan, President & CEO, Broadcom Inc. FQ4 2018 earnings call, 2018-12-06
filingqualifies
Our software licenses have standalone functionality from which customers derive benefit, and the customer obtains control of the software when it is delivered or made available for download. We believe that for the majority of software arrangements, customers derive significant benefit from the ongoing support we provide. Certain of our subscriptions and services arrangements permit our customers to unilaterally terminate or cancel these arrangements at any time at the customer’s convenience, referred to as termination for convenience provisions, without substantive termination penalty and receive a pro-rata refund of any prepaid fees. Accordingly, we account for arrangements with these termination for convenience provisions as a series of daily contracts, resulting in ratable revenue recognition of software revenue over the contractual period.
— Broadcom Inc., Broadcom Inc. Form 10-K, fiscal year 2024 (year ended 3 November 2024), 2024-11-03
filingsupports ✦ best
We recognize software products revenue for the upfront license portion of software arrangements sold. Our software arrangements primarily consist of fees, which may be paid either at contract inception or in installments over the contract term, that provide customers with a right to use the software, access general support and maintenance, and utilize our professional services. Our software licenses have standalone functionality from which customers derive benefit, and the customer obtains control of the software when it is delivered or made available for download. Certain of our software arrangements permit our customers to unilaterally terminate or cancel these arrangements at any time at the customer’s convenience, referred to as termination for convenience provisions, without substantive termination penalty and receive a pro-rata refund of any prepaid fees. For software arrangements without termination for convenience provisions, we recognize revenue for the license portion of the agreements upfront upon transfer of control to the customer, referred to as upfront license revenue, within products revenue. For software arrangements with termination for convenience provisions, we account for these arrangements as a series of daily contracts, resulting in ratable revenue recognition of software revenue over the contractual period, and include them within subscriptions and services revenue.
— Broadcom Inc., Broadcom Inc. Form 10-K, fiscal year 2025 (year ended 2 November 2025), 2025-11-02
filingsupports
Net revenue from our semiconductor solutions segment increased due to strong demand for our networking solutions, primarily custom AI accelerators and AI networking products. Net revenue from our infrastructure software segment increased primarily due to strong demand for our VCF product, including license revenue recognized on contracts where customers do not have the right to terminate and the transition to a subscription license model.
— Broadcom Inc., Broadcom Inc. Form 10-K, fiscal year 2025 (year ended 2 November 2025), 2025-11-02
filingsupports
Many of our existing software customers have multi-year enterprise software license agreements, some of which involve substantial aggregate fee amounts. These customers often do not have a contractual obligation to purchase additional solutions and may have the right to terminate. The failure or inability to renew customer agreements of similar scope, on terms that are commercially attractive to us, could materially adversely affect our business, financial condition, operating results and cash flow. In addition, software license agreements under which customers do not have the right to terminate could cause variations in revenue recognized in each period and our operating results fluctuate from time to time.
— Broadcom Inc., Broadcom Inc. Form 10-K, fiscal year 2025 (year ended 2 November 2025), 2025-11-02

filingqualifies
Licenses for on-premises software provide the customer with a right to use the software as it exists when made available to the customer. Customers may purchase perpetual licenses or subscribe to licenses, which provide customers with the same functionality and differ mainly in the duration over which the customer benefits from the software. Revenue from distinct on-premises licenses is recognized upfront at the point in time when the software is made available to the customer.
— Microsoft Corporation, Microsoft Corporation Form 10-K, fiscal year 2026 (year ended 30 June 2026), 2026-06-30

filingsupports ✦ best
We focus on maintaining an efficient global supply chain and a variable, low-cost operating model. Accordingly, we outsource a majority of our manufacturing operations, utilizing third-party foundry and assembly and test capabilities, as well as some of our corporate infrastructure functions. The majority of our front-end wafer manufacturing operations is outsourced to external foundries, including Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company Limited (“TSMC”). We use third-party CMs for a significant majority of our assembly and test operations, including TSMC, Advanced Semiconductor Engineering, Inc., Foxconn Technology Group, Amkor Technology, Inc. and Siliconware Precision Industries Co., Ltd. We use our internal fabrication facilities for products utilizing our innovative and proprietary processes, such as our FBAR filters for wireless communications and our vertical-cavity surface emitting laser and side emitting lasers based on gallium arsenide (“GaAs”) and indium phosphide (“InP”) lasers for fiber optic communications, while outsourcing commodity processes such as standard CMOS.
— Broadcom Inc., Broadcom Inc. Form 10-K, fiscal year 2025 (year ended 2 November 2025), 2025-11-02

presentationsupports ✦ best
Now, due to the inherent complexity of chip design in advanced nodes, it's not possible for a company to reach efficiency and quality when they're doing just 2-3 chips per node. It takes 2-3 chips in each technology node just to find the basic problems and to try to fix them. By the time a company gets to a semi-proven formula to build chips in a given node, it's time for them to move on to the next node or the next architecture. In this industry of custom ASICs, it is imperative to have scale and continuity so that you can create leverage, efficiency and quality. With our scale in Broadcom, we've been able to perfect this ASIC machine, and we leverage this investment and methods for 50-90 chips with the same platform.
— Frank Ostojic, SVP and General Manager of ASIC Products Division, Broadcom custom silicon accelerators webinar hosted by Deutsche Bank (Status Update), 2022-04-19
presentationsupports
What we do is we take our ASIC machine flow that we have built over four decades, and, for example, in 2018, we port this ASIC machine from 7 nm to 5 nm. Then we use it in this 5 nm ASIC machine flow in the first 2 to 3 chips, and we naturally find problems and inefficiencies. We fix these problems, we eliminate the inefficiencies. By the time we're in the fourth chip, we have an improved flow that is fully adapted to 5 nm. Then we use this proven flow for all the rest of the chips. Whether we're doing those chips in California, here in Colorado, in India or Europe, we use the same proven flow with our proven IP.
— Frank Ostojic, SVP and General Manager of ASIC Products Division, Broadcom custom silicon accelerators webinar hosted by Deutsche Bank (Status Update), 2022-04-19
presentationsupports
As Frank was at pains to lay out his business model, his technical model in ASICs, there's a lot of barriers to entry in doing ASICs. It's not someone showing up one day and saying, I'm going to do a chip in 5. As Frank said, But you haven't done really much in 7. What makes you think you can do it in 5 nm? I'm talking particularly to some competitors or hopeful competitors who say that. It doesn't. It's continuity. You do 7, and when you do 7, you learn from doing 10. When you did 10, you learn from doing 16. Just to do 5, you better have done 7. There is continuity and what they call sustainability. You just can't show up one day and do one. There are lots of barriers to entry in this industry, much as it may not seem so, and Frank outlined it very clearly. Each of them is necessary, maybe not sufficient, to win in this space.
— Hock Tan, President and CEO, Broadcom custom silicon accelerators webinar hosted by Deutsche Bank (Status Update) — Q&A on ASIC competition and customer disintermediation, 2022-04-19
presentationsupports

Merchant AI networking is a standard-parts business with a real standards-based network effect: Ethernet's value to a buyer rises with how many other buyers use it, because switch, NIC, optics and cable vendors all target the same standard, and Broadcom benefits from every hyperscaler that wants an alternative to a single vendor.

The closest public comparables are Cadence Design Systems and Synopsys, whose advantage is also a silicon IP library plus a design methodology re-earned at each process node and amortized across many customers, and whose incumbency comes from a customer's unwillingness to re-qualify a proven flow. filingsupports
Winning a product design does not guarantee sales to a customer. Customers could accelerate, delay or cancel plans, use their own products, purchase products from our competitors, fail to qualify our products, reduce or discontinue use of our products, or fail to successfully market and sell their products, which could reduce demand for our products and cause us to hold a material amount of excess inventory, materially adversely affecting our business, financial condition and results of operations. In addition, we may also be unable to materially recoup our costs or resell our products to other customers due to the custom nature of certain products.

filingsupports
Our semiconductor business is dependent on us winning competitive bid selection processes, known as “design wins.” These selection processes are often lengthy in time and can require us to dedicate significant development expenditures and scarce engineering resources in pursuit of a single customer opportunity. Failure to obtain a particular design win may prevent us from obtaining design wins in subsequent generations of a particular product. This can result in lost revenue and can weaken our position in future selection processes.
— Broadcom 10-K FY2025, 2025-11-02

presentationsupports
Now, due to the inherent complexity of chip design in advanced nodes, it's not possible for a company to reach efficiency and quality when they're doing just 2-3 chips per node. It takes 2-3 chips in each technology node just to find the basic problems and to try to fix them. By the time a company gets to a semi-proven formula to build chips in a given node, it's time for them to move on to the next node or the next architecture. In this industry of custom ASICs, it is imperative to have scale and continuity so that you can create leverage, efficiency and quality. With our scale in Broadcom, we've been able to perfect this ASIC machine, and we leverage this investment and methods for 50-90 chips with the same platform.
— Frank Ostojic, SVP & GM, ASIC Products Division, Broadcom custom-silicon / AI accelerators webinar (Deutsche Bank), 2022-04-19

In custom accelerators the customer owns the architecture, funds the programme and can re-bid each generation, so price is negotiated per generation.

filingqualifies
We perform an annual impairment review of our goodwill during the fourth fiscal quarter of each fiscal year, and more frequently if we believe indicators of impairment exist. The process of evaluating the potential impairment of goodwill is highly subjective and requires significant judgment. To review for impairment, we first assess qualitative factors to determine whether events or circumstances lead to a determination that it is more likely than not that the fair value of any of our reporting units is less than its carrying amount. ... After assessing the totality of events and circumstances, if we determine that it is not more likely than not that the fair value of any of our reporting units is less than its carrying amount, no further assessment is performed.
— Broadcom Inc. Form 10-K, fiscal year 2025 (year ended 2 November 2025), 2025-11-02

So even if I get very good net corporate silicon gross margin on my compute, logic chip on multipliers, there's no way I can apply that kind of add-on margin to the high-bandwidth memory, which is a big part of the cost of the total chip. And so naturally, by simple math, it will -- that whole -- an entire consolidated AI accelerator brings a gross margin below what a traditional silicon product we have out there. No going away from that because you are adding on memory, even though we have to create the access, the IOs that attach in, we do not and could not justify adding that kind of margin to memory. What we do is we take our ASIC machine flow that we have built over four decades, and, for example, in 2018, we port this ASIC machine from 7 nm to 5 nm. Then we use it in this 5 nm ASIC machine flow in the first 2 to 3 chips, and we naturally find problems and inefficiencies. We fix these problems, we eliminate the inefficiencies. By the time we're in the fourth chip, we have an improved flow that is fully adapted to 5 nm. Then we use this proven flow for all the rest of the chips. Whether we're doing those chips in California, here in Colorado, in India or Europe, we use the same proven flow with our proven IP.
— Frank Ostojic, SVP and General Manager, ASIC Products Division, Broadcom / Deutsche Bank custom silicon accelerators webinar (Status Update), 2022-04-19
presentationsupports
Now, due to the inherent complexity of chip design in advanced nodes, it's not possible for a company to reach efficiency and quality when they're doing just 2-3 chips per node. It takes 2-3 chips in each technology node just to find the basic problems and to try to fix them. By the time a company gets to a semi-proven formula to build chips in a given node, it's time for them to move on to the next node or the next architecture. In this industry of custom ASICs, it is imperative to have scale and continuity so that you can create leverage, efficiency and quality. With our scale in Broadcom, we've been able to perfect this ASIC machine, and we leverage this investment and methods for 50-90 chips with the same platform. No ASIC provider in the industry has this scale, this continuity, this efficiency, and this predictability.
— Frank Ostojic, SVP and General Manager, ASIC Products Division, Broadcom / Deutsche Bank custom silicon accelerators webinar (Status Update), 2022-04-19
presentationsupports
It's continuity. You do 7, and when you do 7, you learn from doing 10. When you did 10, you learn from doing 16. Just to do 5, you better have done 7. There is continuity and what they call sustainability. You just can't show up one day and do one. There are lots of barriers to entry in this industry, much as it may not seem so, and Frank outlined it very clearly. Each of them is necessary, maybe not sufficient, to win in this space.
— Hock Tan, President and CEO, Broadcom / Deutsche Bank custom silicon accelerators webinar (Status Update), 2022-04-19
presentationsupports
Custom ASICs is a tough market. Many companies have tried to gain scale in this market, and they have come, and now they're gone. We were able to outlast our competitors. It took us many years to earn our leadership position... In the beginning, we had to compete with IBM Microelectronics, with ST, TI, and others.
— Frank Ostojic, SVP and General Manager, ASIC Products Division, Broadcom / Deutsche Bank custom silicon accelerators webinar (Status Update), 2022-04-19
presentationsupports
See in this chart that our scale in Broadcom enables us to tape out more than 200 chips a year. That is the scale that gives us a huge advantage... You can see that we have more than 20 designs in 5 nm, and we already have 10 designs in development in 3. These numbers are growing every month.
— Frank Ostojic, SVP and General Manager, ASIC Products Division, Broadcom / Deutsche Bank custom silicon accelerators webinar (Status Update), 2022-04-19
presentationsupports
In this chart here, I want you to look at this point right here. That's when the XPU officially starts. What we've been able to do, and let me show you a couple of examples here. We have pre-qualified for thermal, mechanical, and electrical, the packages that we need... It's the same flow we've been using, but we've been improving for a decade.

The chip's front end is designed by Annapurna with the PCIe SerDes licensed from Synopsys. Alchip does the back end physical design and package design... With the Anita variant, Alchip procures chip components from TSMC directly, and Annapurna procures chip components directly for Mariana. The majority of volumes will be for Mariana. While Alchip had similar levels of design involvement for Mariana, the revenue they will see from Mariana should be lower than for Anita.
— SemiAnalysis (Dylan Patel et al.), AWS Trainium3 Deep Dive - A Potential Challenger Approaching, 2025-12-04

filingcontradicts
During fiscal year 2025, approximately 95% of the wafers manufactured by our CMs were produced by TSMC. We believe our wafer requirements represent a meaningful portion of TSMC’s total production capacity. However, TSMC also fabricates wafers for other companies, including some of our competitors, and could choose or be required to materially prioritize capacity for other customers or reduce or eliminate deliveries to us on short notice. In addition, TSMC has raised, and may in the future raise, their prices to manufacture our wafers.
— Broadcom Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025 — Item 1A ranks foundry first, not third: the filing treats TSMC concentration as the operating risk and says nothing equivalent about memory allocation, 2025-12-18

During fiscal year 2025, we purchased approximately two-thirds of our manufacturing materials from five materials suppliers, some of which are single source suppliers. The lead time needed to identify and qualify a new supplier is typically lengthy and there is often no readily available alternative source.

Peer: Micron

filingsupports ✦ best
AI-driven memory and storage growth is outpacing industry supply. In the third quarter of 2026, we continued to benefit from substantial improvements in pricing and margins, reflecting strong demand growth, driven in large part by the continued advancement of AI. The AI-driven growth in the data center has accelerated demand for memory and storage at a rate greater than our ability and the industry’s ability to increase supply. This has led to decisions on supply allocation that may impact certain customers and end markets as the overall market demand for memory and storage exceeds overall industry supply. Robust overall DRAM and NAND demand and constrained supply has led to increased pricing and improved the profitability across our portfolio.
— Micron Form 10-Q for the quarter ended May 28, 2026 — Industry Conditions; a supplier rationing output is the definition of a binding constraint, and it is the only layer in the chain saying so in a filing, 2026-06-25
filingsupports
Strategic customer agreements are structured as take-or-pay agreements, with binding commitments for specific volumes over the multi-year contract terms. Pricing for most agreements is either fixed, or is subject to minimum and maximum pricing. The largest agreements generally have a ceiling price for existing products that approximates the market price in the second calendar quarter of 2026, and a floor price through the term of the agreement. A minority of the agreements do not have any fixed pricing or price bands, as pricing for those agreements is subject to market conditions.
— Micron Form 10-Q for the quarter ended May 28, 2026 — memory is now sold on take-or-pay with a floor, which is what a supplier does when it, not the buyer, holds the constraint, 2026-06-25
